What do they mean?\" --options '[\"file: resolves against the remote URL (fetched from the same server); nested https:// includes allowed; a URL cycle exits 4\",\"Remote files may contain https:// includes but a file: include in remote content exits 4\",\"Remote files may contain no includes at all (any include string in remote content exits 4)\",\"Other\"]'", "description": "Open first discovery decision moment"}
assistant: **Question 1 of ~3: includes inside a remote file**\n\nSuppose the local file contains `team: https://cfg.example.com/team.yaml`, and the fetched `team.yaml` contains its own include strings, for example `prompt: file:prompts/sys.md` or `extra: https://other.example.com/x.yaml`. The contract doesn't say what those mean.\n\n- **(A) Recommended:** a `file:` string in remote content is resolved relative to the remote URL, so it becomes `https://cfg.example.com/prompts/sys.md` and is fetched from the same server. Nested `https://` includes are allowed. A cycle of URLs exits 4, just like a file cycle today. Everything reached this way is remote, so it's read-only. This matches how relative `file:` paths already work: relative to the file that contains them.\n- **(B)** Remote content may contain `https://` includes, but a `file:` include inside it exits 4. A shared file pointing at "a path on your disk" has no clear meaning.\n- **(C)** Remote content may contain no includes at all; any include string in it exits 4. This is the strictest and simplest option.\n- **(D)** Something else.\n\nEither way, I'll treat values from remote content as read-only, and `--in-include` under a remote include will exit 3.\n\nWAITING_FOR_DISCOVERY_INPUT

## arm-a1-002
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; method agent decision resolve 01M4BMKNXT8SSZTQ8ZX7H562MN --mission troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5 --final-answer \"Agent choice (out of contract, product owner 2026-10-07): option A — a file: include inside fetched content resolves against the remote URL (RFC 3986) and is fetched; nested https:// and http:// includes are allowed; a URL cycle exits 4; everything reached through a remote include is remote and read-only.\" ; sed -n 1,142p src/troupe/cli.py; sed -n 1,40p src/troupe/reconcile.py", "description": "Resolve decision; read CLI and reconcile"}
assistant: I'll go with option A and record it in the spec as agent choice S-1, with the reasoning, under decision `01M4BMKN…` (now resolved). Most of what remains is also outside the contract, so I've made those calls myself rather than asking about each one. Here they are for one round of confirmation before I write the spec.\n\n## Intent Summary (draft for confirmation)\n\n**Actor and trigger:** a theatre owner whose team shares configuration from a URL. Example: `team: https://cfg.example.com/team.yaml`, or `coder: {$ref: https://…/coder.yaml, temperature: 0.3}`. They run `troupe config get|show|set|watch` and keep a JSON Schema at `.troupe/troupe.schema.json`.\n\n**Outcome:** remote values read like local ones, but troupe never changes them. A `set` on one exits 3 and stderr shows the include string exactly as written locally, so the owner knows which line to edit. Settings the schema marks `"format": "uri"`, and strings tagged `!literal`, are plain strings, never includes. The loaded configuration must match the schema. `set` writes a string that looks like an include as `!literal …` unless `--include` is given.\n\n**Rule that must always hold:** no troupe command ever writes, or causes a write to, remote content. Every `set` that is refused (exit 2/3/4) changes no file and records no event.\n\n### Agent choices (out of contract, each recorded with its reason)\n\n- **S-1 Includes inside remote content:** option A, as described in my question.\n- **S-2 Fetching:**\n  - Every load fetches fresh, with no cache; each URL is fetched at most once per load.\n  - 10-second timeout, redirects followed, TLS certificates verified, no credentials sent.\n  - The body must be strict UTF-8. The YAML-or-text decision looks at the URL path only, ignoring query and fragment.\n  - A failed fetch (network error, non-2xx, timeout, bad UTF-8) exits 4, naming the URL and the local file and line.\n- **S-3 Mapping form:** `$ref` may hold a remote include string, with overrides, as for `file:`. A `$ref` value is always an include, so `!literal` on it exits 4.\n- **S-4 Read-only scope:** each of these exits 3, and stderr contains the include string as written in the local file and tells the owner to change that line:\n  - changing an existing remote value (YAML or text)\n  - replacing a whole remote include\n  - `--in-include` into remote content\n  - appending to a remote list, or adding a key inside a remote list item\n  - `--expand-alias` of an alias in remote content\n\n  For nested remote includes, the outermost local include line is reported. Values from local overrides stay writable (step 5).\n- **S-5 New keys:** under a remote include they become local overrides, with scalar-to-mapping conversion as in step 5. The step-6 alias refusal still applies.\n- **S-6 History and watch:**\n  - Remote leaves are history leaves like any other. A reconcile records upstream changes as file-lineage events.\n  - `watch` does not poll URLs. It reconciles when local files change, and also when the schema file changes.\n  - `show --sources` attributes remote scalars to the resolved URL.\n- **S-7 Schema lookup:**\n  - A string's path is walked through `properties[key]`, else `additionalProperties` (when it's a schema object), and `items` for list indexes.\n  - Nothing else is followed (`$ref`, `allOf`, `patternProperties`, `prefixItems`). Only `"format": "uri"` counts.\n  - For an aliased string, the path where the anchored node is written decides.\n- **S-8 Schema handling:**\n  - The schema is read fresh on every load. If it isn't valid JSON, isn't a valid 2020-12 schema, or uses a `$ref` that isn't a local fragment, loading exits 4 and names the schema file.\n  - `format` is an annotation and is not checked.\n  - The schema validates the resolved configuration: includes, overrides, merge keys and aliases are all resolved first.\n  - A failure exits 4, naming the schema file and the first failing path. No schema file means no validation.\n- **S-9 `set` validity:** a `set` whose result would fail validation exits 3, names the path and the schema file, and changes nothing.\n- **S-10 `!literal` rules:**\n  - Allowed only on scalars, which become strings. On a mapping or list it exits 4; other tags still exit 4.\n  - `get`/`show` print the plain string without the tag.\n  - Tags in a `set` VALUE are still refused with exit 2.\n- **S-11 How `set` writes include-looking strings:**\n  - The rule applies to every string in VALUE, including strings nested in a mapping or list VALUE, each judged at its own path.\n  - Such a string is written as `!literal <value as step 1 would write it>`.\n  - If the replaced value had `!literal` and the new VALUE is a string, the tag is kept.\n  - A local text include's content is file text, not YAML, so it is never tagged.\n- **S-12 `--include`:**\n  - Only `set` accepts it; elsewhere it exits 2. On a string that doesn't look like an include, it has no effect.\n  - If the new include can't be read or fetched, the `set` exits 3.\n  - If the schema marks PATH as `format: uri`, `--include` exits 3, because the value would be a literal anyway.\n- **S-13 Step-1 behaviour change:** `set b file:p.md` now writes `b: !literal file:p.md`. The existing test asserting that this creates an include will need `--include`. The contract requires this change.\n\nEverything else from steps 1–6 stays as it is. I don't need a decision on any of these unless one looks wrong to you.\n\nDoes this summary look right? Reply "confirmed" or tell me which choices to change.\n\nWAITING_FOR_DISCOVERY_INPUT

That branch is merged into `main` once the mission is accepted. `setup-plan` created an empty `plan.md` for me to fill in. While I map the code in the background, I have one planning question.\n\n**Planning question 1: how to check the configuration against the JSON Schema**\n\nS-8 needs a full JSON Schema 2020-12 check: validating the configuration, checking the schema file itself against the 2020-12 meta-schema, and following `$ref`s that point inside the schema file. Today the project has only two runtime dependencies, `eventsourcing` and `ruamel.yaml`.\n\n- **(A) Recommended: add `jsonschema`.** It is the standard Python implementation of 2020-12, which it validates against the official test suite, and is published on PyPI. It brings in `attrs`, `referencing`, `jsonschema-specifications` and `rpds-py`. `rpds-py` is a compiled Rust extension, but PyPI ships prebuilt wheels for it on macOS and Linux, so installing doesn't build anything. I'd pin it to a known version range in `uv.lock`, the same way the current dependencies are pinned. With it I can also check the schema file against the 2020-12 meta-schema, turn off network lookups of references (only `#…` fragments allowed), and leave `format` unchecked (S-8). Fetching URLs needs no new dependency: Python's standard library is enough.\n- **(B) Write our own validator.** It would cover the 2020-12 rules (`type`, `properties`, `required`, `enum`, `items`, `$ref` and so on) with no new dependencies. It's a lot of code to get right, and a validator that is subtly wrong would accept or reject configurations incorrectly.\n- **(C) Something else.**\n\nWAITING_FOR_PLANNING_INPUT

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/tests; grep -rn \"^def _\\|^def test_.*tag\\|tags are not\\|anchors and tags\\|byte-surgically\\|file:p.md\\|becomes an include\\|include target not found\" . | head -80; ls ../src/troupe", "description": "Find test helpers and relevant tests"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/tests; grep -rln \"_set_ok\\|_set_fails\\|def _set\\b\\|def _refused_set\\|def _set(\" .; grep -rn \"def _set\\w*\\|def _refus\\w*\\|def _ok\\w*\\|def _fails\\w*\" integration | head -30; grep -rn \"file:\" integration/test_set_existing.py | head -20", "description": "Find set helper patterns and include-string set tests"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/tests; sed -n 1,75p integration/test_set_existing.py; sed -n 480,525p integration/test_set_existing.py; grep -rn \"file:\" integration/test_set_new_entries.py | head", "description": "Read set_existing helpers and include-string tests"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; ls spec/; ls spec/*remote* 2>/dev/null; grep -rln \"file:p.md\\|becomes an include\\|file:missing\" tests | head; grep -rn \"is_dir\\|exists()\" src/troupe | head", "description": "List specs and related tests"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/spec/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5; wc -l spec.md plan.md; ls research tasks decisions; grep -n \"S-[0-9]*\\b\" spec.md | head -40", "description": "Inspect remote-includes spec state"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; grep -n '^name = ' uv.lock; cat src/troupe/__main__.py; sed -n 1,30p spec/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5/plan.md", "description": "Check locked deps and plan stub"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/src/troupe; sed -n 98,135p writeback/aliases.py; sed -n 262,300p writeback/aliases.py; sed -n 470,530p writeback/collections.py; sed -n 762,800p writeback/collections.py", "description": "Read alias copy rendering and collections refusal spots"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/src/troupe; grep -rn \"yamlcore.compose\\|compose(\" --include=*.py . | grep -v \"def compose\"; sed -n 187,235p writeback/collections.py", "description": "Find all compose callers"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/tests; grep -n \"# from\\|include target not found\\|include cycle\" integration/test_show_sources.py integration/test_loading.py | head -12; sed -n 25,60p unit/test_loader_aliases.py; grep -n \"def test\" integration/test_watch_includes.py | head", "description": "Sample test assertions for sources and load errors"}
assistant: I've mapped all seven areas below. Paths are under `/work/repo/src/troupe/` unless shown in full. The spec for this step is already written at `spec/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5/spec.md`, with decisions S-1 to S-13 at lines 39–51 and acceptance scenarios at lines 80–169. `plan.md` there is still the unfilled template.\n\nFive findings will shape the plan most:\n- **Every `SourceFile` is a local file.** Reads, writes, `watch` polling, cycle detection, include lookup and `show --sources` all treat `SourceFile.path` as a `Path` on disk.\n- **A tagged scalar's span starts at the tag.** Any in-place write to a `!literal` value will drop or corrupt the tag unless `compose` changes.\n- **Three helpers call `compose` and would break on `!literal` files** if only the loader is taught the tag.\n- **Read-only checks have no single home.** About eight write paths need a "not remote" check.\n- **No JSON Schema library and no HTTP client are installed.** Dependencies are only `eventsourcing` and `ruamel.yaml`.\n\n## 1. Include rule, resolution, `$ref`, node types\n\n**Constants** (`loader.py:70-74`): `INCLUDE_PREFIX = "file:"`, `YAML_SUFFIXES = (".yml", ".yaml")`, `BOM`, `SHOWN_MAX = 40`. The rule is documented in the module docstring at lines 7-11.\n\n**Where the include decision is made:**\n- **Scalar form:** `_Loader._content` at `loader.py:198-202`. It resolves the value (`resolve_plain` for plain style), then `if isinstance(value, str) and value.startswith(INCLUDE_PREFIX): return self._include(value[len(INCLUDE_PREFIX):], origin, segments)`. `segments` (the merged config path) is already available here, which is where a schema `format: uri` lookup would plug in.\n- **Two more copies of the same test** must stay in sync:\n  - `_is_include_string` (`loader.py:400-402`), used by `_holds_include` (405-420) for the C3 check that anchors may not sit on includes.\n  - `_ref_include` (`loader.py:301-324`).\n\n**`$ref` mapping form:**\n- `_Loader._mapping` (`loader.py:211-261`) finds a `$ref` key (`raw_key.text == REF_KEY`, any quoting). It refuses `<<` together with `$ref` (226-231).\n- `_ref_include` (301-324) does the D-1 check: an anchored `$ref` value is refused, and the value must be a string starting with `file:`. Otherwise: `"cannot load {file.path}: {dotted(..$ref)} must be an include string ('file:...'), got {_shown(...)}"`.\n- The holder `MappingNode` is set as `include.holder` (246). A holder with only `$ref` returns the include itself (247-248).\n- A `TextInclude` with overrides is a load error (249-253). So is a YAML include whose content is not a mapping (254-260).\n- Otherwise the result is `deep_merge(include, holder, ref=include, ...)` (261).\n\n**`_Loader` state** (`loader.py:118-127`):\n- `_overrides: dict[Path, str]` (resolved paths)\n- `_files: dict[Path, SourceFile]` (cache; one object per path per load, and placement relies on that identity)\n- `_stack: list[Path]` (cycle detection)\n- `_anchored: dict[int, Node]` (per file)\n\n**Methods:**\n- `read_source(path)` (77-92): resolves the path, reads bytes, strict UTF-8. Errors read `"cannot read {resolved}: ..."`.\n- `load(config_file, overrides)` (95-110) builds `_Loader(...).load_root`.\n- `_source(path)` (132-142): `path.resolve()`, then the cache, then the override or `read_source`.\n- `_load_yaml(file, segments)` (144-161): cycle check `if file.path in self._stack` → `"include cycle at {dotted}: a -> b -> a"` (`str(p)`). It pushes onto the stack, swaps `_anchored`, calls `yamlcore.compose(file.text, str(file.path), allow_anchors=True)`, and gives an empty document an empty `MappingNode`.\n- `_include(target_text, origin, segments)` (368-388) resolves relative to the containing file: `including = origin.file.path; target = including.parent / target_text`.\n  - Missing target: `if not target.exists() and target.resolve() not in self._overrides` → `"include target not found: {target} (included from {including}, at {dotted})"`.\n  - Suffix in `YAML_SUFFIXES` → `YamlInclude(origin, target=self._source(target), root=self._load_yaml(...))`.\n  - Directory → `"include target is a directory: ..."`.\n  - Anything else → `TextInclude(origin, target=self._source(target))`.\n\n**Node types** (`tree.py`):\n- `SourceFile` (29-40; `eq=False`, identity semantics): **`path: Path`** (absolute, resolved) and `text: str`. This is the only field holding a file location, and it would need to hold a URL.\n- `Origin(file: SourceFile, start, end)` (43-58), frozen, with `.source`.\n- `Anchor(name, origin, path: tuple[str,...])` (61-73).\n- `ScalarNode(origin, value, style, anchor)` (76-83). No tag or literal flag exists today.\n- `MappingEntry(key, key_origin, key_style, value)` (86-93).\n- `MappingNode(origin, flow, entries, anchor)` (96-107).\n- `SequenceNode` (110-117).\n- `YamlInclude(origin, target: SourceFile, root, holder)` (120-134).\n- `TextInclude(origin, target: SourceFile, holder)` (137-148).\n- `MergedMapping(base, override, ref)` (151-166).\n- `MergeKeyMapping(written)` (169-180).\n- `AliasNode(origin, name, target, anchored, merge, written)` (183-207).\n- `Node` union (213).\n- `Scalar`, `ScalarStyle` (25-26).\n\n`Origin.file` is the only path to file identity, via `include.target.path` and `origin.file.path`.\n\n## 2. How tags are refused today; scalar style and spans\n\n**`yamlcore.compose(text, source, *, allow_anchors=False)`** (`yamlcore.py:179-202`) walks ruamel parser events in `_Composer` (214-348).\n- `_Composer._node` (276-296): `if event.tag is not None: self._refuse(event, "tags are not supported")`.\n- `_refuse` (334-345): with `allow_anchors` false, every refusal becomes `"anchors and tags are not supported"`. With it true, the tag message stays and anchor/alias refusals are skipped.\n- The first refusal is stored and raised after the document parses: `single_document` (249-250) raises `LoadError("cannot parse {source}: {refusal} at line N")`.\n\n**Raw nodes** (94-150): `RawScalar(text, style, start, end, anchor)`, `RawMapping`, `RawSequence`, `RawAlias`. **No tag field.** `_content` (298-315) builds a `RawScalar` from `event.start_mark/end_mark`, with `_STYLES` (74-80) mapping to plain/single/double/literal/folded.\n\n**Span facts:**\n- Anchored nodes get `start` moved to the content (`_from_content_start` 359-365, `_content_start` 368-383). `_anchor_token` (351-356) already handles a tag before the anchor ("refused anyway").\n- **Tagged scalars get no such adjustment.** I checked with ruamel: in `a: !literal file:x.md`, the ScalarEvent `start_mark` is at the `!` (index 3), so the span includes `!literal `. For `c: &x !literal y` the start is at `&x`, and `_from_content_start` would land on `!literal`.\n\nConsequences if tags are allowed without a fix:\n- `ReplaceScalar`/`_replace_span` would delete the tag. S-11 requires keeping it.\n- `blocks.parse_block(text, start, …)` expects `start` at `|`/`>`.\n- `aliases._Renderer.scalar` copies `node.origin.source` verbatim (`writeback/aliases.py:268-281`), so a copy would keep the tag.\n- `collections._refuse_properties` (`writeback/collections.py:1103-1147`) already refuses converting an include that has "an anchor or tag".\n\nSuggested approach: mirror `RawAnchor` with a `RawTag`/tag field, and give `ScalarNode` a tag or `literal` flag.\n\n**Other `compose` callers that must tolerate `!literal`:**\n- `writeback/collections.py:196` (`detect_indent`, with `allow_anchors=True`). On `LoadError` it silently falls back to the default step.\n- `writeback/collections.py:484-485` (`_check_open_end`). A `LoadError` would propagate here.\n- `writeback/scalars.py:148` (`_round_trips_plain`, default mode, which is fine).\n- `writeback/apply.py:469` (`_include_string`, default mode: a tagged include string returns `None`).\n\n**VALUE parsing** (`values.py:25-43`): `parse_value` calls `yamlcore.compose(text, source=f"VALUE {text!r}")` in default mode, so anchors, aliases and tags all raise `LoadError`, re-raised as `UsageError` (exit 2). `_is_block` (46-51) refuses block nodes; `_convert` (54-67) builds `Value`.\n- `scalar_of` (96-107): a `TextInclude` reads as `target.text`.\n- Tests that pin the current messages:\n  - `tests/unit/test_values.py:109-114` (`"!t x"` → "anchors and tags are not supported")\n  - `tests/unit/test_yamlcore.py:207-212, 399-401`\n  - `tests/unit/test_loader_aliases.py:238` (`"a: !t 1"` → "tags are not supported at line 1")\n\n## 3. The `set` flow\n\n**`commands/set_value.py`** (whole file, 89 lines), `run` at 50-89:\n1. `parse_path`, then `parse_value` (exit 2 before any load).\n2. `with open_session(theatre, reconcile=True) as session:`\n3. `plan = apply_set(session.tree, segments, path, value, raw_value, in_include=..., expand_alias=...)`. `plan.tree is None` means a no-op.\n4. `changes = diff(flatten(session.tree), flatten(plan.tree))`.\n5. `write_files(plan.files)`. An `OSError` becomes `WriteRefused(f"{path}: cannot write {names}: ...")` with `names` built from `str(file.path)` (86).\n6. `session.history.record(changes, cli_lineage(actor, reason))`.\n\nA new `--include` flag needs a parser entry in `cli.py:86-101` (beside `--in-include`/`--expand-alias`; `set_` subparser only, so other commands give exit 2 automatically). It is then threaded through `run` → `apply_set` → `place`/renderers.\n\n**`writeback/apply.py`:**\n- `WritePlan(files: dict[SourceFile, str], tree)` (138-149).\n- `apply_set` (152-200):\n  - `place(...)`.\n  - `ReplaceText` → `_plan_text`.\n  - No-op via `_matches(placement.node, value)` (188-189).\n  - `ExpandAlias` → `_unchanged` then `_plan_expansion`.\n  - Otherwise `_edits(placement, ...)`, then `apply_edits`. If the text is unchanged → `_refuse`.\n  - `_verify(root, segments, dotted, {file: new_text}, lambda node: _matches(node, value))`.\n- `_plan_expansion` (212-276) re-loads in memory with `load(config_file, overrides=texts)` (`texts: dict[Path,str]`, keyed by `file.path`).\n- `_plan_text` (302-327): the text-include write, with the CRLF rule. It writes `{target: content}`, i.e. the include *target file*. A remote target must be refused here or earlier.\n- `_edits` (333-369) dispatches:\n  - `ConvertInclude` → `convert_include_edits`\n  - `AddKeys` → `new_key_edits`\n  - `AppendItem` → `new_item_edits`\n  - `ReplaceCollection` → `replace_value_edits`\n  - `ReplaceScalar` → `_scalar_edits` (377-411), which uses `render_scalar(value, _QUOTE_STYLES.get(node.style), context)` and `_replace_span` (414-426), replacing exactly `origin.start:end`.\n- `_matches` (432-456) treats an include node as equal to its written string via `_include_string` (466-472).\n  - **Note:** an existing `!literal file:x` scalar and `set --include p file:x` would compare equal and become a no-op unless `_matches` learns about the include/literal distinction.\n- `_verify` (475-512) is research R-17: `load(config_file, overrides={file.path: text})`, then `walk`. A `LoadError`/`PathError` → `_refuse(dotted)`. Then read-back, anchor-name and `_same_outside` checks.\n  - `_refuse` (562-563): `WriteRefused(f"{dotted}: cannot write this change byte-surgically; no file changed")`.\n  - Schema validation failure (S-9, exit 3 naming PATH and the schema file) must be a distinct `WriteRefused`, not swallowed into the generic `_refuse` at line 502.\n- `_same_outside` (566-684) compares include identity by `target.path` (636, 643). `_gains_override` (710-738) compares `ref.target.path` and `base.origin.file.path`. `_ref_path` (741-742) returns `Path | None`.\n\n**Step-1 "set b file:p.md becomes an include" and the missing-target refusal:**\n- There is no dedicated code. The VALUE string is rendered as an ordinary scalar by `collections._Renderer.attach`. `render_scalar` (`writeback/collections.py:399`) handles new keys, `render_flow_value` (635-647) handles flow, and `_scalar_edits` handles replacements.\n- The file then becomes an include simply because `_verify` re-loads it.\n- This is documented in the apply.py docstring at lines 70-71: "A string `file:...` written as a value becomes an include when the files are read again; it is verified as written text (the include string)".\n- A missing target makes the re-load raise `LoadError` ("include target not found") inside `_verify`. That is caught at `apply.py:502` → `_refuse` → "cannot write this change byte-surgically".\n- Tests:\n  - `tests/integration/test_set_existing.py:506-511` (`test_value_naming_a_missing_include_target_is_refused`, asserting `"byte-surgically"`)\n  - `:514-525` (`test_value_naming_an_existing_text_file_becomes_an_include`). S-13 changes this one to need `--include`.\n\n**Where `!literal ` would be emitted:** every scalar renderer for new values.\n- `_Renderer.attach` (`writeback/collections.py:365-399`, with mapping/sequence/item at 401-420).\n- `render_flow_value`/`_flow_entries` (635-656), `flow_append_edits` (658+), `_replace_in_flow` (989+), `convert_include_edits` override lines (1019-1100).\n- `apply._scalar_edits`, plus the multi-line paths `blocks.replace_with_multiline` (`writeback/blocks.py:220`), `render_new_multiline` (175) and `replace_block` (342).\n- Each string must be judged at its own resulting path (S-11), so the renderers need the path, or a per-path predicate, passed down.\n- `scalars.render_scalar` (`writeback/scalars.py:130-134`) is the common choke point for one-line strings, but it is path-agnostic.\n\n**`writeback/placement.py`** decides every refusal:\n- `place` (201-287):\n  - D-6 `$ref` segment → exit 3 (230-233).\n  - `walk`.\n  - `_route_aliases` → `_through_alias` (409-441). Without `--expand-alias` the message includes `{file.path} line N` (433).\n  - Existing path → `_existing`.\n  - List append → `AppendItem` (246-256).\n  - `<<` refusal.\n  - `MergedMapping` → `_on_base` (`--in-include`) / `_on_side`.\n  - `in_include` → `AddKeys(_written(deepest))` (275-276).\n  - Otherwise `_include_ref` → holder `_on_side` or `_convert` → `ConvertInclude`.\n- `_existing` (444-477):\n  - YAML-include replacement refused: `"{dotted}: value comes from include {node.target.path}; refusing to replace it ..."` (447-451).\n  - `MergedMapping` with ref (452-457); base/override D-10 (458-463); `MergeKeyMapping` (464-468).\n  - **`TextInclude` → `ReplaceText`** (469-470), the text-include write.\n  - Otherwise `ReplaceScalar`/`ReplaceCollection`.\n- `_include_ref` (480-509) matches `node.target is file` by `SourceFile` identity; `_peel` (512-522).\n- `_on_side` (525-548), `_on_base` (551-563), `_convert` (566-572).\n\n**Remote read-only checks (S-4) are needed at:**\n- `_existing` (`ReplaceScalar`/`ReplaceCollection`/`ReplaceText` whose node's `origin.file` or target is remote; whole-value replacement of a remote `YamlInclude`).\n- `AppendItem` (250).\n- `AddKeys` with `in_include` (276) and `_on_base` (563).\n- The list-rule `AddKeys` when `_include_ref` returns `None` because of a list (506-507).\n- `_through_alias` with `expand_alias` for an alias in remote content.\n\nThe error must quote the *outermost* include string as written in the local file, which needs the route (`found.nodes`) to find the first remote `YamlInclude`/`TextInclude` and its `origin.source`.\n\n## 4. Session, theatre, lock, reconcile, watch, `show --sources`\n\n- **`theatre.py`:** `STATE_DIR_NAME=".troupe"`, `ROOT_FILE_NAMES` (11-12). `Theatre(directory, state_dir, config_file)` (15-27). `locate` (30-54). The schema path `<theatre>/.troupe/troupe.schema.json` would sit beside these, as `state_dir / "troupe.schema.json"`.\n- **`session.py`:** `open_session(theatre, *, reconcile)` (44-75) takes `theatre_lock(theatre.state_dir)` (`lock.py:23`), runs `current = locate(...)` then `tree = load(current.config_file)`, opens `HistoryStore`, then imports or reconciles. A schema load and validation (exit 4) belongs here or inside `loader.load`. `apply._verify`/`_plan_expansion` call `loader.load` directly, so putting the schema in `load` covers verification too, but it then needs the schema path or theatre.\n- **`reconcile.py`:**\n  - `reconcile` (33-43) works on leaves only.\n  - `watched_files(tree) -> dict[Path, bytes]` (46-62): `_add` (65-66) does `files.setdefault(file.path, file.text.encode())`. `_collect` (69-88) adds `node.origin.file` for every node and `node.target` for includes.\n  - Remote sources must be excluded here, and the schema file added (S-6).\n- **`commands/watch.py`:**\n  - `Baseline = dict[Path, bytes | None]` (30).\n  - `_reconcile_and_snapshot` (93-106) builds the baseline from `watched_files` plus both root candidates.\n  - `_current`/`_read` (109-118) call `path.read_bytes()` on every baselined key, so a URL key would fail.\n  - Poll loop at 68-90.\n- **`show --sources`:**\n  - `commands/show.py:40-46`: `sources_root = session.theatre.directory`, then `yamlout.to_text(render(node), sources_root)`.\n  - `yamlout.Line(text, source: Path | None)` (62-73).\n  - `_scalar_lines` sets `source = node.target.path` (TextInclude) or `node.origin.file.path` (176-178).\n  - `_line_text` (118-121) appends `" # from " + source_label(...)`.\n  - `source_label(path, theatre_dir)` (99-105) is `PurePath(os.path.relpath(path, theatre_dir)).as_posix()`. It must print a URL verbatim (S-6).\n- **`show` and `!literal`:** `show` renders strings via `_quote_style`, and a `file:…`/`https://…` literal would be printed plain. The spec (S-10) only requires no `!literal` in the output.\n- **History:** `history.py`/`leaves.py` store only config paths and values. Nothing there is file-based.\n\n## 5. Error messages that format file paths\n\n**`loader.py`:**\n- `read_source` 87, 91\n- cycle 148 (`str(p)` chain)\n- anchor-on-include 178, 310\n- merge+`$ref` 229\n- overrides on non-YAML 251-252 (`include.target.path`)\n- non-mapping content 258-259\n- bad merge value 297\n- D-1 319\n- key errors 341, 346, 352\n- missing target 375-376, directory 385-386 (both name `target` and `including`)\n- `yamlcore.compose` messages use `source=str(file.path)` (154), giving `"cannot parse {source}: …"` (`yamlcore.py:197, 200, 250, 273`)\n\n**`writeback/`:**\n- `placement.py:433` (alias `in {file.path} line N`), `449`, `455`, `458-462`\n- `apply.py:265-266`, `314`\n- `aliases.py:127`\n- `collections.py:488`, `909`, `1078`, `1145`\n\n**Elsewhere:** `set_value.py:86` and `theatre.py:47-54`.\n\nFetch errors (S-2) must name both the URL and the local including file. The `origin.file` of the include string gives the local file.\n\n## 6. Test infrastructure\n\n**`tests/conftest.py`:**\n- `run_cli(*args, cwd, env, unset) -> CliResult(code, out, err)` (46-69) runs **subprocesses** (`[sys.executable, "-m", "troupe", ...]`) and strips `TROUPE_THEATRE`/`TROUPE_ACTOR`.\n- `snapshot(theatre)` (82-88) returns bytes of every file except history. `all_files` (77-79).\n- `history_events`, `core`, `stored_records`, `recorded_within`.\n- `WatchProcess` (130-232): a real `watch` child with reader threads, `wait_ready`, `wait_stderr`, `stop`.\n- Fixture `make_theatre(files: dict[str, str|bytes]) -> Path` (235-254) writes under `<tmp>/theatre/.troupe/`. `MakeTheatre` type at 28.\n\n**Remote tests:** a local HTTP server (e.g. `http.server` in a thread, bound to 127.0.0.1) in the test process is reachable from the subprocess CLI. No such fixture exists yet. The 10 s timeout case needs a server that never answers.\n\n**Helper patterns are defined per module:**\n- `tests/integration/test_set_existing.py:26-65`:\n  - `_set(theatre, path, value)`\n  - `_get`\n  - `_bytes(files)` (prefixes `.troupe/`)\n  - `_set_ok(theatre, path, value, expected: Files, shown)`: exit 0 and empty output, exact `snapshot`, then `get`\n  - `_set_fails(theatre, path, value, code, names)`: code, empty stdout, a single `troupe: ` stderr line containing `names`, snapshot unchanged\n- Variants:\n  - `test_set_aliases.py:20-42` (`_cli`, `_set_ok` → events, `_refused`)\n  - `test_aliases_acceptance.py:64-72`\n  - `test_set_new_entries.py:20` and `test_set_overrides.py:44` (`_set(theatre, path, value, *flags)`)\n  - `test_set_in_include.py:25`\n  - `test_set_history.py:41-45`\n- Unit-level loader tests call `loader.load` directly (`tests/unit/test_loader_aliases.py:30-62`: `_load`, `_at`, `_scalar`, `_refused`). Placement unit tests: `test_placement_overrides.py:43-76`, `test_placement_aliases.py:49-91`.\n\n## 7. Everything that assumes `SourceFile` is a local `Path`\n\n- **`tree.SourceFile.path: Path`** and the `Anchor`/`Origin` users.\n- **`loader`:**\n  - `read_source`; `_source` (`path.resolve()`, `_files` keyed by `Path`).\n  - `_overrides: dict[Path,str]` (`.resolve()` at 124).\n  - `_stack: list[Path]`.\n  - `_include` uses `including.parent / target_text`, `.exists()`, `.suffix`, `.is_dir()`. A URL needs `urljoin`, and the URL path suffix with query/fragment ignored (S-1, S-2).\n  - `load(config_file: Path, overrides: Mapping[Path,str])`.\n- **`writeback/fileio.py`:** `write_files` (120-128) calls `atomic_write(source.path, text)`, and `atomic_write` (93-117) uses `path.stat()`, `path.parent`, `path.name`. The loader cache (`_files`), the in-memory override map, the cycle stack and `load(..., overrides=...)` are all keyed by `Location`. The history event schema and the on-disk files under `.troupe/` other than the new optional schema file are unchanged.\n\n## Types\n\n| Type | Module | Fields / meaning | Invariants |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `Url` (new) | `tree.py` | `text: str`, the absolute URL (`https://…` or `http://…`) as written, or resolved with `urljoin` (S-1) | frozen; equality is by text |\n| `Location` (new alias) | `tree.py` | `Path \\| Url` | a `Path` is absolute and resolved |\n| `SourceFile` (changed) | `tree.py` | `location: Location` (was `path: Path`), `text: str`; property `remote` | one object per location per load; a remote `SourceFile` is never in a `WritePlan` |\n| `RawTag` (new) | `yamlcore.py` | span of the `!literal` token and its trailing whitespace | only on `RawScalar`, only in file mode |\n| `RawScalar` (changed) | `yamlcore.py` | `+ tag: RawTag \\| None`; `start` is the content start (after any tag and anchor) | |\n| `ScalarNode` (changed) | `tree.py` | `+ literal: Origin \\| None`, the span of the `!literal` tag | `value` is a `str` when `literal` is set |\n| `YamlInclude` / `TextInclude` (unchanged shape) | `tree.py` | `target: SourceFile`, which may now be remote | an include whose `origin.file` is remote has a remote target (S-1) |\n| `Schema` (new) | `schema.py` | `location: Path`, `document: dict`, validator | the schema has passed the 2020-12 meta-schema check; every `$ref`/`$dynamicRef` is a `#` fragment |\n| `Violation` (new) | `schema.py` | `path: str` (dotted or `(root)`), `message: str` | the first violation in JSON-path order |\n| `Loaded` (new) | `configuration.py` | `tree: Node`, `schema: Schema \\| None`, `fetched: dict[Url, str]` | `tree` validates against `schema` |\n| `LiteralStr` (new) | `includes.py` | a `str` subclass marking a VALUE string to be written `!literal` | only created by `mark_literals` |\n| `FetchError` (new) | `errors.py` | a `LoadError` (ex...[truncated 2434 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "# CLI contract as delivered: Remote includes and schema (step 7)\n\nContract text (verbatim from the product owner, step 7):\n\n- A string starting `https://` or `http://` is also an include; it is fetched when the configuration loads (YAML if the URL path ends `.yml`/`.yaml`, otherwise text). A failed fetch → exit 4.\n- Changing a value that comes from a remote include → exit 3; stderr contains the include string exactly as written in the local file. (New keys under a remote include become local overrides, per step 5.)\n- `<dir>/.troupe/troupe.schema.json` (JSON Schema 2020-12), if present, describes the configuration. A string whose schema — found through `properties`, `items` and `additionalProperties` — has `\"format\": \"uri\"` is a literal, not an include. A string tagged `!literal` is a literal.\n- The loaded configuration must validate against the schema, else exit 4. A `set` that would make it invalid → exit 3.\n- `set PATH VALUE` where `VALUE` is a string that would be read as an include and the schema does not mark `PATH` as `format: uri` writes it tagged `!literal`, unless `--include` is given.\n\n## Command surface\n\n```\ntroupe config set [--reason TEXT] [--in-include] [--expand-alias] [--include] PATH VALUE\n```\n\n`--include` is accepted only by `set`, anywhere among its options (S-12).\n\n| Exit | New causes in step 7 |\n|---|---|\n| 0 | Value set; include-looking strings are written `!literal …` unless `--include` is given or the schema marks their path `uri` (S-11, S-13). |\n| 2 | `--include` on `get`/`show`/`history`/`watch`; any tag (including `!literal`) in VALUE. |\n| 3 | Changing remote content (S-4); a result that fails the schema (S-9); `--include` where the new include cannot be read or fetched, or where its path is marked `uri` (S-12). |\n| 4 | Failed fetch (S-2); URL cycle (S-1); invalid schema file (S-8); configuration fails the schema (S-8); `!literal` on a mapping, list or `$ref` value, or any other tag (S-10, S-3). |\n|...[truncated 2531 chars]
tool: Write {"content": "# Quickstart: remote includes and a schema (manual walkthrough)\n\nRun from the repository root. A local server stands in for the team's configuration host.\n\n```bash\nT=$(mktemp -d); mkdir -p \"$T/.troupe\" \"$T/srv\"\nprintf 'model: opus\\ntemperature: 0.7\\n' > \"$T/srv/team.yaml\"\n(cd \"$T/srv\" && python3 -m http.server 8765 --bind 127.0.0.1) &   # stop with: kill %1\ncat > \"$T/.troupe/troupe.yml\" <<'EOF'\nteam: http://127.0.0.1:8765/team.yaml\nwebhook: https://hooks.example.com/notify\nEOF\ncat > \"$T/.troupe/troupe.schema.json\" <<'EOF'\n{\"$schema\": \"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema\",\n \"type\": \"object\",\n \"properties\": {\"webhook\": {\"type\": \"string\", \"format\": \"uri\"},\n                \"team\": {\"type\": \"object\", \"properties\": {\"model\": {\"type\": \"string\"}}}}}\nEOF\nexport TROUPE_THEATRE=\"$T\"\n\nuv run troupe config get team.model                 # opus (fetched)\nuv run troupe config get webhook                    # https://hooks.example.com/notify (not fetched: format uri)\nuv run troupe config show --sources                 # team.* attributed to http://127.0.0.1:8765/team.yaml\nuv run troupe config set team.model sonnet; echo $? # 3, stderr shows http://127.0.0.1:8765/team.yaml\nuv run troupe config set team.temperature 0.3       # becomes a local override ($ref form)\nuv run troupe config set docs https://x.example/y   # written as  docs: !literal https://x.example/y\nuv run troupe config set team.model 5; echo $?      # 3: an override would not match the schema\ncat \"$T/.troupe/troupe.yml\"\nkill %1; uv run troupe config get team.model; echo $?   # 4: failed fetch names the URL and troupe.yml\n```\n", "file_path": "/work/repo/spec/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5/quickstart.md"}

Today that test is copied in three places in `loader.py`. The module decides:\n  - what an include string is (`file:`, `https://`, `http://`);\n  - where it points: `file:` resolves against the containing file or URL (S-1);\n  - whether it is YAML or text;\n  - whether a string is a literal instead (`!literal`, or the schema marks its path `format: uri`).\n- **Fetching.** A source location becomes `Path | Url`. assistant: The plan for step 7 is done and committed on `feat/troupe-config-remote-includes`, and `setup-plan` now reports the plan phase complete. Nothing has been merged into `main` yet, no product code has changed, and nothing was pushed.\n\n**Branch:** we're on `feat/troupe-config-remote-includes`, which is also where the mission's work merges (`branch_matches_target: true`). That branch is merged into `main` locally once the mission is accepted.\n\n**Validator decision:** as you directed, I took the safest option and recorded it as decision `01M4BMXJ…`. The plan adds `jsonschema` (2020-12), because a hand-written validator that was subtly wrong would accept or refuse configurations against the contract. To limit the risk of the new packages:\n- the schema check never fetches anything outside the schema file;\n- `rpds-py`, its compiled dependency, is never built from source;\n- `research.md` has the supply-chain table.\n\n**How the design hangs together:**\n- **One place for the include rule.** It's currently copied in three places in the loader; a new module will decide what counts as an include, where it points, and whether it's YAML or text.\n- **Fetching.** It uses only the Python standard library: a 10-second total deadline, redirects only to http/https, verified certificates. Each URL is fetched once per command, so a `set` checks its result against the same remote content it started from.\n- **Remote content stays read-only.** One check refuses any change to it and quotes the include line as written. It runs before the alias refusal, and again on the exact files a `set` is about to write. File writes also assert that every target is a local file.\n- **`!literal` tags.** The YAML layer will record where a tag sits, so rewriting a value keeps the tag. Without this, a rewrite would silently delete it.\n- **Schema checks.** A configuration that fails the schema exits 4 when it loads, and a `set` that would make it fail exits 3. `watch` also reacts to changes to the schema file.\n\n**Artifacts** (in `spec/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5/`):\n- `plan.md`: summary, how the plan meets each charter rule, the files to add or change, 7 implementation areas (IC-01 to IC-07), and risks\n- `research.md`: design decisions R-1 to R-13, the supply-chain review, and a challenge pass (7 findings, each accepted, changed or deferred with a reason)\n- `data-model.md`, `contracts/cli.md` (exit codes and message wording), `quickstart.md`\n\nCommits: `66d8e75` (plan) and `72281e4` (the other artifacts plus the decision record).\n\nThings to know before implementation:\n- **Challenge pass:** I reviewed the design alone; the multi-agent review the planning instructions call for was deferred, and `research.md` says why.\n- **Slow tests:** the timeout tests take about 10 seconds each.\n- **HTTPS test:** the one that checks certificates are verified needs `openssl` installed, and is skipped with a reason if it isn't.\n- **No size limit:** nothing caps how large a fetched remote file can be. The spec doesn't ask for a limit, so I didn't invent one.\n\nNext is `/method-command` to turn these areas into work packages; I've stopped here until you run it.

tool: Edit {"file_path": "/work/repo/spec/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5/tasks/WP01-locations-fetch-harness.md", "new_string": "**`errors.py`**: add `class FetchError(LoadError)` (exit 4 is inherited). Its constructor is `FetchError(url: str, reason: str)`; it keeps both as attributes and builds the message `cannot fetch {url}: {reason}`. Its docstring says the message names the URL. The loader (WP05) adds the including file and the path by raising a new `FetchError`-compatible `LoadError` whose message is `cannot fetch {url} (included from {including}, at {path}): {reason}` (contract table). That is why `reason` must be available separately. `fetch` itself names only the URL and the reason. `errors.py` must not import `location.py` (keep it at the bottom of the graph), so pass `str(url)`.\n\nIn the bullets below, `FetchError(f\"cannot fetch {url}: X\")` is shorthand for `FetchError(str(url), \"X\")`.", "old_string": "**`errors.py`**: add `class FetchError(LoadError)` (exit 4 is inherited). assistant: The work packages for step 7 are written, validated and committed on `feat/troupe-config-remote-includes`. Nothing is merged into `main` yet and nothing was pushed. Validation found no ownership overlaps or dependency problems, and all 12 functional requirements are mapped to at least one package.\n\n**10 packages, 47 subtasks** (`spec/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5/tasks.md`):\n\n| WP | What it does | Subtasks | Depends on |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| WP01 | URL type, fetching, local HTTP test server | 5 | — |\n| WP02 | `!literal` in the YAML parser | 4 | — |\n| WP03 | Schema module and the `jsonschema` dependency | 5 | — |\n| WP04 | The include rule in one module | 4 | WP01, WP03 |\n| WP05 | Loader: remote includes and literal strings | 5 | WP01, WP02, WP04 |\n| WP06 | Load-and-validate entry point, sessions, `watch`; reading stories end to end | 6 | WP03, WP05 |\n| WP07 | Writing `!literal` in new values | 4 | WP04, WP05 |\n| WP08 | `set`: remote content read-only, schema checks, `--include` | 8 | WP06, WP07 |\n| WP09 | Independent end-to-end tests of the `set` story (US6) and `--include` with URLs | 3 | WP08 |\n| WP10 | Acceptance tests, quickstart run, docs agreement, all four gates | 3 | WP09 |\n\n- **Size:** prompts run from about 120 to 260 lines. None exceeds 10 subtasks or 700 lines, and every prompt starts with the "Do This First: Load Agent Profile" section.\n- **Parallel work:** WP01, WP02 and WP03 can start together, and WP06 and WP07 can run side by side after WP05. Each package has its own lane (10 lanes).\n- **Smallest useful slice:** WP01–WP06 deliver reading remote includes, literal strings and schema validation. WP07–WP08 add the write rules.\n- **Profiles:** every package is assigned `python-pedro` running on `claude`, and is linked to a new tracking issue, `repo`.\n\n**Plan changes made while writing the packages** (committed separately with reasons):\n- **Field name kept:** `SourceFile.path` keeps its name but can now hold a URL as well as a file path. mypy still flags every place that needs a file on disk, and packages don't need to edit each other's files for a rename.\n- **Copies keep `!literal`:** a `!literal` value copied by `--expand-alias` now stays literal. Before this fix the copy would have become a remote include.\n- **Second marker:** strings written with `--include` are now marked separately, so the "nothing changed" check can tell an include from a literal with the same text.\n- **Error message unchanged:** tags other than `!literal` keep the step-6 message, so existing tests don't change.\n\n**Known exceptions to file ownership:**\n- WP05 makes a small type-only edit in `apply.py`, which WP08 owns.\n- WP08 updates the two step-1 tests that the new `!literal` default (S-13) changes.\n\n**Commits:** `8992702` (requirement mapping), `5558c76` (tasks), `b52e9dc` (plan amendments), `fcb3564` (status bookkeeping).\n\n**Next:** `/method-command` has to run first; implementation refuses to start until its report exists.\n\nShould I use the `/method-implement-review` skill to implement and review all ten packages through to completion? It would run `/method-command` first, then dispatch implementing and reviewing agents for each package, handle rejections, and merge the lanes when done. You can also pick which models implement and review, limit it to a subset (for example WP01–WP03), or just have me run `/method-command` now so you can read the consistency report before deciding.

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5-lane-b && cat > /tmp/probe_wp02.py <<'EOF'\nfrom ruamel.yaml import YAML\ncases = [\"a: !literal file:x.md\\n\", \"c: &x !literal y\\n\", \"c: !literal &x y\\n\", \"a: !literal\\nb: 1\\n\", \"[!literal ]\\n\", \"[!literal , b]\\n\", \"a: !literal\\n\", \"a: !literal\", \"a: !literal |\\n  x\\n\", \"a: !<!literal> x\\n\", \"a: !literal # c\\n  v\\n\", \"a: !literal\\n  [1]\\n\", \"a: !literal {b: 1}\\n\", \"{u: !literal https://x}\\n\",\"a: !literal  'q'\\n\", \"a: !literal 'q'\\n\", \"a: !literal\\n  - x\\n\", \"- !literal\\n- x\\n\",\"a: !literal,\\n\"]\nfor t in cases:\n    try:\n        evs = list(YAML(typ=\"safe\", pure=True).parse(t))\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(repr(t), \"ERR\", e); continue\n    print(repr(t))\n    for e in evs:\n        if hasattr(e, \"tag\") and (e.tag or getattr(e,'anchor',None)):\n            print(\"   \", type(e).__name__, e.tag, e.anchor, e.start_mark.index, e.end_mark.index, repr(getattr(e,'value',None)), getattr(e,'style',None))\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/probe_wp02.py"}

tool: Bash {"command": "mkdir -p /tmp/x && cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5-lane-a && cat > src/troupe/location.py <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Where a configuration source lives: a local ``Path`` or a remote ``Url`` (research R-1).\n\nThis module deliberately imports nothing from ``troupe``. ``tree.py``, ``includes.py`` and\n``remote.py`` all import it, so it must sit at the bottom of the import graph; any ``troupe``\nimport here would risk an import cycle.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Url:\n    \"\"\"An absolute http(s) URL, as written or as resolved by ``urljoin`` (spec S-1).\n\n    The text is never normalised (no lower-casing, no trailing-slash changes): equality and\n    hashing go by the exact text, and messages show exactly what the user wrote or what\n    ``urljoin`` produced. ``str(url)`` is that text, so an f-string message such as\n    ``f\"cannot load {file.path}: ...\"`` prints the URL unchanged when ``path`` is a ``Url``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    text: str\n\n    def __str__(self) -> str:\n        return self.text\n\n\ntype Location = Path | Url\nEOF\ncat >> src/troupe/errors.py <<'EOF'\n\n\nclass FetchError(LoadError):\n    \"\"\"A remote include could not be fetched; the message names the URL (TROUPE-003).\n\n    ``url`` and ``reason`` are kept separately so the loader can re-raise with the including\n    file and the configuration path added: ``cannot fetch {url} (included from ..., at ...):\n    {reason}`` (contracts/cli.md). ``url`` is a plain ``str`` so this module stays free of\n    ``troupe`` imports.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, url: str, reason: str) -> None:\n        super().__init__(f\"cannot fetch {url}: {reason}\")\n        self.url = url\n        self.reason = reason\nEOF"}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Fetching remote includes: the only network access in troupe (spec S-2, research R-2).\n\n``fetch(url)`` performs one HTTP(S) ``GET`` with the standard library and returns the body as\ntext. The rules, each enforced here and nowhere else:\n\n- **Request**: a plain ``GET``. No credentials and no cookies are ever sent (the opener has no\n  auth handler and no cookie processor). Proxies from the environment are honoured, as urllib\n  does by default.\n- **TLS**: ``ssl.create_default_context()``, so certificates and host names are verified.\n  Verification is never disabled.\n- **Redirects**: followed only to ``http``/``https`` targets; any other scheme is refused.\n  urllib's cap on the number of redirects stays in force.\n- **Deadline**: one total deadline of ``FETCH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`` for the whole fetch, redirects\n  included (NFR-004). See ``_Deadline`` for how it is enforced.\n- **Status**: any 2xx is success (a 204 yields ``\"\"``); everything else is a failure.\n- **Body**: strict UTF-8, no newline translation, and a leading BOM stays in the text, exactly as\n  ``loader.read_source`` treats local files. A ``charset`` in ``Content-Type`` is ignored: S-2\n  says the body must be UTF-8.\n\nEvery failure raises ``FetchError`` (exit 4) whose message is ``cannot fetch <url>: <reason>``;\nno other exception escapes ``fetch``. Nothing is cached here: fetching each URL once per\ncommand is the loader's job.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport http.client\nimport io\nimport socket\nimport ssl\nimport time\nimport urllib.request\nfrom collections.abc import Buffer\nfrom typing import IO, Any, Final\nfrom urllib.error import HTTPError, URLError\nfrom urllib.parse import urljoin, urlsplit\n\nfrom troupe.errors import FetchError\nfrom troupe.location import Url\n\nFETCH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS: Final = 10.0\n_CHUNK: Final = 65536\n_ALLOWED_SCHEMES: Final = frozenset({\"http\", \"https\"})\n_TIMED_OUT: Final = f\"timed out after {FETCH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS:g} s\"\n\n\ndef fetch(url: Url) ->...[truncated 7997 chars]
tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"A real HTTP(S) server for remote-include tests, run in a thread of the test process (R-13).\n\nTests register routes (path -> status, body, headers, behaviour), point troupe at ``url(path)``\nand read the request log to assert what was fetched, how often and with which method. The server\nlistens on ``127.0.0.1`` only, so no test touches the outside network (C-006); the CLI subprocess\nstarted by ``run_cli`` reaches it the same way.\n\nBehaviours beyond a normal response:\n\n- ``hang``: accept the request and send nothing for 30 s (ended early by ``close()``).\n- ``trickle``: send ``200`` with a large ``Content-Length``, then one body byte per second until\n  the client goes away or the server is closed.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport ssl\nimport threading\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer\nfrom typing import Final, Literal, cast\nfrom urllib.parse import urlsplit\n\nHANG_SECONDS: Final = 30.0\nTRICKLE_LENGTH: Final = 1_000_000\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass Route:\n    \"\"\"How the server answers one path.\"\"\"\n\n    status: int = 200\n    body: bytes = b\"\"\n    headers: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)  # e.g. {\"Location\": \"...\"}\n    behaviour: Literal[\"normal\", \"hang\", \"trickle\"] = \"normal\"\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Request:\n    \"\"\"One logged request.\"\"\"\n\n    method: str\n    path: str  # path + query exactly as requested\n\n\nclass _Server(ThreadingHTTPServer):\n    daemon_threads = True\n\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\n        super().__init__((\"127.0.0.1\", 0), _Handler)\n        self.routes: dict[str, Route] = {}\n        self.log: list[Request] = []\n        self.lock = threading.Lock()\n        self.stop = threading.Event()\n\n\nclass _Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):\n    def _answer(self) -> None:\n        server = cast(_Server, self.server)\n        with server.lock:\n            server.log.append(Request(self.command, self.path...[truncated 3623 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5-lane-b && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/unit/test_yamlcore.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace(\"\"\"    RawSequence,\n    compose,\"\"\",\"\"\"    RawSequence,\n    RawTag,\n    compose,\"\"\",1)\ns+='''\n\n# --- !literal on scalars (allow_anchors=True; spec S-10, research R-4) ----------------------------\n\n\ndef _tagged(text: str, key: str = \"a\") -> RawScalar:\n    scalar = _value(text, key)\n    assert isinstance(scalar, RawScalar)\n    assert scalar.tag is not None\n    return scalar\n\n\ndef _tag_text(text: str, scalar: RawScalar) -> str:\n    assert scalar.tag is not None\n    return text[scalar.tag.start : scalar.tag.end]\n\n\ndef test_literal_tag_on_plain_scalar_spans_tag_and_content_starts_after_it() -> None:\n    text = \"a: !literal file:x.md\\\\n\"\n\n    scalar = _tagged(text)\n\n    assert scalar.tag == RawTag(start=3, end=text.index(\"f\"))\n    assert _tag_text(text, scalar) == \"!literal \"\n    assert (scalar.start, scalar.end) == (text.index(\"f\"), text.index(\"\\\\n\"))\n    assert (scalar.text, scalar.style) == (\"file:x.md\", \"plain\")\n\n\ndef _same_span_as_without_tag(value: str, tag: str = \"!literal \") -> RawScalar:\n    plain = _value(f\"a: {value}\\\\n\", \"a\")\n    tagged = _tagged(f\"a: {tag}{value}\\\\n\")\n\n    assert (tagged.start, tagged.end) == (plain.start + len(tag), plain.end + len(tag))\n    assert tagged.tag == RawTag(start=3, end=3 + len(tag))\n    return tagged\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize((\"value\", \"style\"), [(\"'it''s'\", \"single\"), ('\"x\\\\\\\\ty\"', \"double\")])\ndef test_literal_tag_on_quoted_scalars(value: str, style: str) -> None:\n    scalar = _same_span_as_without_tag(value)\n\n    assert scalar.style == style\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    (\"value\", \"indicator\"), [(\"|\\\\n  line\\\\n\", \"|\"), (\">-\\\\n  folded\\\\n\", \">\"), (\"|+\\\\n  k\\\\n\\\\n\", \"|\")]\n)\ndef test_literal_tag_on_block_literal_and_folded...[truncated 7060 chars]

By default\nthey are refused with ``anchors and tags are not supported at line N``, exactly as before the\nswitch existed (``values.parse_value`` and the indentation probe rely on this). With\n``allow_anchors=True`` they are composed; an undefined alias, a recursive alias (one inside the\nnode its anchor is attached to) and any tag are refused (spec C2, FR-002). Refusals, like\nbefore, are raised only once the whole document parsed, so a parse error anywhere wins, and the\nfirst refusal in document order is reported.\n''','''Anchors, aliases and tags are decided in one place, ``compose``'s ``allow_anchors`` switch\n(\"file mode\" when true). By default (VALUE mode) every anchor, alias and tag is refused with\n``anchors and tags are not supported at line N``, exactly as before the switch existed\n(``values.parse_value`` relies on this). In file mode anchors and aliases are composed; an\nundefined alias and a recursive alias (one inside the node its anchor is attached to) are refused\n(spec C2, FR-002). tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"The configuration schema: loading and checking ``troupe.schema.json``, the S-7 ``format: uri``\nlookup and 2020-12 validation of plain data (spec S-7, S-8; research R-6).\n\nEvery test writes a real schema file under ``tmp_path / \".troupe\"`` and loads it through\n``load_schema``, so the checks run against the file the user would have written.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom troupe.errors import LoadError\nfrom troupe.schema import SCHEMA_FILE_NAME, Schema, Step, Violation, load_schema\n\n\ndef _state_dir(tmp_path: Path) -> Path:\n    state_dir = tmp_path / \".troupe\"\n    state_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)\n    return state_dir\n\n\ndef _write_bytes(tmp_path: Path, content: bytes) -> Path:\n    path = _state_dir(tmp_path) / SCHEMA_FILE_NAME\n    path.write_bytes(content)\n    return path\n\n\ndef _load(tmp_path: Path, document: object) -> Schema:\n    _write_bytes(tmp_path, json.dumps(document).encode(\"utf-8\"))\n    schema = load_schema(_state_dir(tmp_path))\n    assert schema is not None\n    return schema\n\n\ndef _refusal(tmp_path: Path, content: bytes) -> str:\n    path = _write_bytes(tmp_path, content)\n    with pytest.raises(LoadError) as caught:\n        load_schema(path.parent)\n    assert caught.value.message.startswith(f\"cannot load schema {path}: \")\n    return caught.value.message.removeprefix(f\"cannot load schema {path}: \")\n\n\n# --- load_schema -------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_absent_schema_file_gives_none(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    assert load_schema(_state_dir(tmp_path)) is pytest.raises(LoadError) as caught:\n        load_schema(path.parent)\n    assert caught.value.message == f\"cannot load schema {path}: not a file\...[truncated 11564 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"The configuration's JSON Schema, ``<state dir>/troupe.schema.json`` (spec S-7, S-8; research R-6).\n\nThis module is the one place that knows about the schema (TROUPE-007). It works on plain JSON\ndata (dicts, lists, scalars) and on path steps; it does not know the tree, files or the loader.\n\n- ``load_schema`` reads and checks the schema file. Any problem is a ``LoadError`` naming it.\n- ``Schema.uri_at`` answers whether the schema marks a configuration path ``\"format\": \"uri\"``.\n- ``Schema.validate`` validates data as JSON Schema 2020-12 and returns the first violation.\n\nOffline by construction (research A-4): every ``$ref``/``$dynamicRef`` must be a fragment within\nthe file, and the validator gets an empty ``referencing.Registry``, so nothing is ever fetched.\n``format`` is an annotation only: the validator has no ``format_checker`` (S-8).\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nfrom collections.abc import Iterator, Sequence\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any, Final, Literal\n\nfrom jsonschema import Draft202012Validator\nfrom jsonschema.exceptions import SchemaError, ValidationError\nfrom referencing import Registry\nfrom referencing.exceptions import Unresolvable\n\nfrom troupe.errors import LoadError\n\nSCHEMA_FILE_NAME: Final = \"troupe.schema.json\"\nDRAFT_2020_12: Final = \"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema\"\n\n# Keywords whose value maps *names* to subschemas: a ``$ref`` key directly inside one of these is\n# a name (e.g. a property called ``$ref``), not a reference keyword.\n_NAME_KEYWORDS: Final = frozenset(\n    {\"properties\", \"patternProperties\", \"$defs\", \"definitions\", \"dependentSchemas\"}\n)\n_REFERENCE_KEYWORDS: Final = (\"$ref\", \"$dynamicRef\")\n\ntype Step = tuple[Literal[\"key\"], str] | tuple[Literal[\"index\"], int]\n\"\"\"One configuration path step; the caller knows whether it is a mapping key or a list index.\"\"\"\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass V...[truncated 8218 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5-lane-c && sed -i 's/_pointer(error.path)/_pointer(error.absolute_path)/; s/for part in error.path)/for part in error.absolute_path)/' src/troupe/schema.py && uv run pytest -q tests/unit/test_schema.py 2>&1 | tail -40", "description": "Run schema tests"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5-lane-c && git diff --stat; grep -n \"Unresolvable\\|absolute_path\" src/troupe/schema.py", "description": "Check current file state"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5-lane-c && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/schema.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace(\"from referencing.exceptions import Unresolvable\",\"from referencing.exceptions import PointerToNowhere, Unresolvable\")\ns=s.replace('''        except Unresolvable as error:\n            raise LoadError(f\"cannot load schema {self.path}: cannot resolve {error.ref}\") from None''','''        except Unresolvable as error:\n            # Safety net: a remote reference cannot get here through ``load_schema``, but a\n            # fragment pointing nowhere (``#/$defs/missing``) is only found when it is used.\n            ref = f\"#{error.ref}\" if isinstance(error, PointerToNowhere) else error.ref\n            raise LoadError(f\"cannot load schema {self.path}: cannot resolve {ref}\") from None''')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\np='tests/unit/test_schema.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('\"not valid UTF-8 at byte 33\"','\"not valid UTF-8 at byte 32\"')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run ruff format src/troupe/schema.py tests/unit/test_schema.py && uv run pytest -q tests/unit/test_schema.py 2>&1 | tail -5; uv run mypy --strict 2>&1 | tail -8", "description": "Fix, format, test, typecheck"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5-lane-c && uv run pytest -q tests/unit/test_schema.py -k unresolvable_local 2>&1 | grep -E \"^E\" | head; uv add --dev \"types-jsonschema>=4.26,<5\" 2>&1 | tail -3; grep -n \"types-jsonschema\" -A3 uv.lock | head -8", "description": "Inspect failure, add type stubs"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5-lane-c && uv run python -c \"\nfrom jsonschema import Draft202012Validator\nfrom referencing import Registry\nv=Draft202012Validator({'properties':{'m':{'\\$ref':'#/\\$defs/missing'}}},registry=Registry())\ntry: list(v.iter_errors({'m':1}))\nexcept Exception as e: print(type(e).__mro__, repr(e.ref), getattr(e,'_wrapped',None).__class__)\n\"; git diff pyproject.toml", "description": "Inspect exception type"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5-lane-c && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/schema.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold='''            ref = f\"#{error.ref}\" if isinstance(error, PointerToNowhere) else error.ref\n'''\nnew='''            # jsonschema wraps referencing's error; a PointerToNowhere's ``ref`` is the bare\n            # JSON pointer, so it is shown as the fragment the user wrote.\n            cause = getattr(error, \"_wrapped\", error)\n            ref = f\"#{error.ref}\" if isinstance(cause, PointerToNowhere) else error.ref\n'''\nassert old in s\ns=s.replace(old,new)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run ruff format src tests >/dev/null; uv run pytest -q 2>&1 | tail -3; uv run mypy --strict 2>&1 | tail -3; uv run ruff check 2>&1 | tail -3; uv run ruff format --check 2>&1 | tail -2", "description": "Fix and run all gates"}

assistant: VERDICT: REJECT\n\n1. **`/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5-lane-c/src/troupe/schema.py:107-108`: a missing anchor gives an error message with nothing after "cannot resolve".**\n   - **What is wrong:** `load_schema` accepts any `$ref`/`$dynamicRef` that starts with `#`, so it accepts `"$ref": "#foo"` even when no `$anchor: foo` exists. That failure only shows up during `validate`. There, jsonschema wraps a `referencing.exceptions.NoSuchAnchor`, and that error has `ref=''` and `anchor='foo'`. The code only special-cases `PointerToNowhere`, so it uses the empty `error.ref`.\n   - **Reproduced:** `Schema(Path('/x/troupe.schema.json'), {'properties': {'m': {'$ref': '#foo'}}}).validate({'m': 1})` raises `LoadError("cannot load schema /x/troupe.schema.json: cannot resolve ")`. A `$dynamicRef: "#nope"` gives the same result.\n   - **Why it blocks:** a normal user-written schema can trigger this, unlike the remote-ref safety net. The message names the file (TROUPE-003) but leaves the reason empty, so it does not match the T013 contract `cannot resolve {ref}`.\n   - **Required:**\n     - Show what the user wrote for every `Unresolvable` cause. For example, when the cause is a `NoSuchAnchor`, show `#{cause.anchor}`. As a general fallback, never print an empty ref.\n     - Read the cause from the public `error.__cause__` (I checked: it is the `NoSuchAnchor`) instead of the private `_wrapped` attribute.\n     - Add a test for a dangling `#anchor` reference in `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5-lane-c/tests/unit/test_schema.py`, next to `test_unresolvable_local_reference_during_validation_is_a_load_error` (line 307).\n\nEverything else meets T010–T014:\n- **T010 (dependency):**\n  - `jsonschema>=4.26,<5` is added, plus `[tool.uv] no-build-package = ["rpds-py"]` with the required comment.\n  - The lock has hashed rpds-py cp312 wheels for manylinux x86_64/aarch64 and macOS.\n  - jsonschema ships no `py.typed`, so adding `types-jsonschema` to the dev group is justified. No other dependency was added.\n- **T011 (`load_schema`):**\n  - The checks run in the specified order, and every failure message names the file.\n  - The 2020-12 `$schema` check and the meta-schema check (including the regex format check) are both on.\n  - The conservative `$ref` walk is documented, and a property named `$ref` is not treated as a reference.\n- **T012 (`uri_at`):** it follows exactly `properties`, `additionalProperties` and `items`, uses explicit `("key"/"index")` steps, and counts only `"format": "uri"`. The docstring has the S-7 wording.\n- **T013 (`validate`):**\n  - It uses `Draft202012Validator` with an empty `Registry()` and no format checker.\n  - The first violation is picked by a deterministic `(0,int)`/`(1,str)` path key, then path length, validator name and message.\n  - The root path is reported as `(root)`.\n- **T014 (tests) and scope:** `schema.py` imports nothing from troupe except `troupe.errors`. All 55 tests in `tests/unit/test_schema.py` pass, and the test names match what they assert. There is no stray scope.\n\nNon-blocking notes:\n- **Test strength:** `test_property_named_dollar_ref_is_not_a_reference` (test_schema.py:144) can't fail. The value under the `$ref` name is a dict, and the walk only checks string values. Since the meta-schema check runs first and requires schema values there, the exemption at schema.py:194 can never be reached through `load_schema`. The test matches what the WP asked for, so this is not a defect. But it does not prove the exemption works, and the `_NAME_KEYWORDS` logic could be simplified or noted as defensive.\n- **Redundant sort key:** in `_order` (schema.py:210), `len(path)` adds nothing, because tuple comparison already puts a prefix path first. It is harmless.\n- **Root pointer wording:** for a meta-schema error at the document root, the message shows `(root)` rather than the RFC 6901 empty pointer. That is a reasonable choice for readability.\n- **Locked versions:** they are in the commit message (jsonschema 4.26.0, referencing 0.37.0, jsonschema-specifications 2025.9.1, rpds-py 2026.9.1, attrs 26.1.0), but not in the WP Activity Log, which T010 asks for. The orchestrator may want to copy them there.

The result is `LoadError(\"cannot load schema …/troupe.schema.json: cannot resolve \")`, and `$dynamicRef: \"#nope\"` gives the same. That does not meet the T013 contract `cannot resolve {ref}`, and the message gives no reason (TROUPE-003 spirit).\n   - **Required:**\n     - Name what the user wrote for every `Unresolvable` cause, e.g. `#{cause.anchor}` for `NoSuchAnchor`. The meta-schema check forces schema values under `properties`, so the `_NAME_KEYWORDS` exemption is unreachable through `load_schema`. tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5-lane-c && cat > /tmp/probe.py <<'EOF'\nfrom jsonschema import Draft202012Validator\nfrom referencing import Registry\nfrom referencing.exceptions import Unresolvable\nimport jsonschema\nprint(jsonschema.__version__ if hasattr(jsonschema,'__version__') else '')\nfor s in [{\"$ref\":\"#foo\"},{\"$dynamicRef\":\"#nope\"},{\"$ref\":\"#/$defs/missing\"}]:\n    try:\n        list(Draft202012Validator(s, registry=Registry()).iter_errors(1))\n    except Unresolvable as e:\n        c=e.__cause__\n        print(type(e).__name__, repr(e.ref), type(c).__name__, vars(c) if c else None)\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/probe.py; uv pip show jsonschema 2>/dev/null | grep Version; grep -n \"Unresolvable\\|_wrapped\\|cannot resolve\" -n tests/unit/test_schema.py"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5-lane-c && cat >> /tmp/probe.py <<'EOF'\nfrom referencing.exceptions import NoSuchAnchor, PointerToNowhere\nprint(NoSuchAnchor.__mro__, PointerToNowhere.__mro__)\ntry:\n    list(Draft202012Validator({\"$ref\":\"#foo\"}, registry=Registry()).iter_errors(1))\nexcept Unresolvable as e:\n    print(repr(e.__cause__), e.__cause__.anchor, isinstance(e, Unresolvable), isinstance(e.__cause__, Unresolvable))\nEOF\nuv run python -W ignore /tmp/probe.py 2>&1 | tail -3; sed -n 295,330p tests/unit/test_schema.py"}

That original error knows what was written: a ``PointerToNowhere``\n    holds the bare JSON pointer in ``ref`` and a ``NoSuchAnchor`` holds an empty ``ref`` and the\n    anchor name, so both are shown as the fragment ``#...``. Never empty: an unknown cause\n    without a ``ref`` is shown by its ``repr``.\n    \"\"\"\n    cause = error.__cause__ if isinstance(error.__cause__, Unresolvable) else error\n    if isinstance(cause, NoSuchAnchor):\n        return f\"#{cause.anchor}\"\n    if isinstance(cause, PointerToNowhere):\...[truncated 1566 chars]

The composer records the\ntag span (RawTag) and starts the scalar's span at its content, after tag and anchor in either\norder, so writers can keep or drop the tag without knowing tag syntax (research R-4).\n!literal on a mapping or list is refused ('!literal applies only to scalars'); every other\ntag keeps the step-6 message, and VALUE mode still refuses every tag (S-11).\" && git log --oneline -1 && method agent tasks mark-status T006 T007 T008 T009 --status done --mission troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5 2>&1 | tail -1\ncd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5-lane-a && find . -name __pycache__ -not -path './.venv/*' -prune -exec rm -rf {} + ; git add -A -- src tests && git commit -q -m \"feat(WP01): Url/Location types, remote.fetch and a local HTTP test server\n\nRemote includes need one place that touches the network (spec S-2, research R-2): fetch()\ndoes a credential-free GET with verified TLS, follows only http(s) redirects, enforces a 10 s\ntotal deadline (also against a trickling body), accepts only 2xx and strict UTF-8 bodies, and\nturns every failure into FetchError(url, reason) (exit 4). Url/Location sit at the bottom of\nthe import graph so the tree can hold URLs (R-1). Tests use a threaded local server with a\nrequest log, a self-signed https fixture, and NO_PROXY set so they never leave the machine\n(C-006).\" && git log --oneline -1 && method agent tasks mark-status T001 T002 T003 T004 T005 --status done --mission troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5 2>&1 | tail -1", "description": "Commit WP02 and WP01 and mark subtasks done"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5-lane-b && mkdir -p /tmp/oldyc/troupe_old && git show method/mission-troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5:src/troupe/yamlcore.py > /tmp/oldyc/old_yamlcore.py && cat > /tmp/oldyc/cmp.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys; sys.path.insert(0,'/tmp/oldyc')\nimport old_yamlcore as O\nfrom troupe import yamlcore as N\ncases = [\n \"a: &x\\nb: 1\\n\", \"a: &x\\n\\n# c\\nb: 1\\n\", \"a: &x # c\\n\\n# d\\nb: 1\\n\", \"a: &x\", \"[&x , b]\\n\", \"[&x ]\\n\",\n \"a: &x\\n  b: 1\\n\", \"a: &x\\n- 1\\n\", \"- &x\\n- 2\\n\", \"a: &x |\\n  l\\n\", \"a: &x 'q'\\n\", \"{a: &x , b: 1}\\n\",\n \"&x a: 1\\n\", \"a: &x # c\\n  v\\n\", \"? &k\\n: v\\n\", \"a: &x\\n  # c\\n  v\\n\", \"a:\\n  &x\\n  # c\\nb: 1\\n\",\n \"- &x\\n  # c\\n- 1\\n\", \"a: &x\\n...\\n\",\n]\ndef dump(n, depth=0):\n    out=[]\n    t=type(n).__name__\n    out.append((t, n.start, n.end, getattr(n,'anchor',None) and (n.anchor.start,n.anchor.end)))\n    if hasattr(n,'pairs'):\n        for k,v in n.pairs: out+=dump(k)+dump(v)\n    if hasattr(n,'items'):\n        for v in n.items: out+=dump(v)\n    return out\nfor c in cases:\n    try: o=dump(O.compose(c,\"f\",allow_anchors=True))\n    except Exception as e: o=repr(e)\n    try: n=dump(N.compose(c,\"f\",allow_anchors=True))\n    except Exception as e: n=repr(e)\n    print(\"SAME\" if o==n else \"DIFF\", repr(c), \"\" if o==n else f\"\\n  old={o}\\n  new={n}\")\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/oldyc/cmp.py"}

assistant: VERDICT: APPROVE\n\nWP02 meets T006–T009. It changes only `src/troupe/yamlcore.py` and `tests/unit/test_yamlcore.py`. All 180 tests in `test_yamlcore.py` pass. The orchestrator had already checked the full suite, mypy and ruff.\n\n**Checked against the requirements**\n- **Tag only on scalars:** `RawTag` and `LITERAL_TAG: Final` are added as specified, and only `RawScalar` gets `tag: RawTag | None = None`. Only `ScalarEvent` nodes are given a tag.\n- **Refusal rules:**\n  - VALUE mode refuses every tag with the old message (`!literal x` and `a: !t 1` both give `anchors and tags are not supported at line 1`).\n  - In file mode, `!literal` on a mapping or sequence (block, flow, or with an anchor in either order) gives `!literal applies only to scalars at line N`.\n  - `!t`, `!!str`, `!<tag:x>`, `!<!literal>`, `!e!literal` under `%TAG`, `!literalx` and the bare `!` all keep `tags are not supported`.\n  - The first refusal in document order still wins, and it is still raised only after the document parses.\n- **Content start:** I tried plain, single- and double-quoted, `|`, `>-`, `|+`, `|2`, CRLF, BOM, `--- !literal x`, flow mappings and sequences, keys (`!literal k: v`, `? !literal`), and a block scalar on the line after the tag. In every case the scalar's `start` is its content start, and for block scalars that is the `|` or `>` indicator.\n- **Empty values:** for `a: !literal`, `a: !literal # c`, `[!literal ]`, `- !literal` and an empty value at end of file, the span has zero width right after the tag, and the tag span is exactly `!literal`.\n- **Tag and anchor in either order:** the content start is the same, and the anchor span is exactly `&x`. This holds across line breaks and comments, and in block, flow and key positions. The search for a tag written before the anchor starts at the end of the previous event, so it is not fooled by `!literal` or `#` inside earlier quoted text or comments. It also works right after nested block mappings, sequences and block scalars, and after `%TAG` directives. Removing the tag span from `!literal &x y` leaves `&x y`.\n- **Text inside the scalar:** a quoted scalar containing `!literal &z` or `&x` keeps its full span.\n- **Untagged spans unchanged:** I ran 19 anchored, untagged inputs (empty values, comments between anchor and content, `?` keys, `...`, flow collections) through the old and new `compose` with `allow_anchors=True`. Every node span and anchor span is identical.\n- **Docstrings and test names:** the module, `compose`, `_Composer` and `RawScalar` docstrings are updated and include the `%TAG` scope note. The test names match what they assert, and all the required tests are present, plus extras for tag-before-anchor, comments and empty-with-anchor.\n\n**Non-blocking notes**\n1. The module docstring (`src/troupe/yamlcore.py`, `RawTag` bullet) says "For an empty value it is exactly the `!literal` token". That is wrong for `a: !literal &x` followed by a newline, where the tag span is `!literal ` and runs up to `&x`. The test `test_empty_literal_tagged_value_followed_by_an_anchor_ends_after_the_anchor` pins the actual behaviour, which is correct. The `RawTag` class docstring ("up to the scalar's content") also leaves out the case where an anchor comes next.\n2. When the tag comes before an anchor that is on a later line (`b: !literal\n  &x\n  {c: 1}`), the refusal names the anchor's line (3), not the tag's line (2). `!t` behaves the same way, so this is existing behaviour, not something WP02 introduced.\n3. The WP prompt said to keep `_from_content_start` for anchor-only nodes. It was replaced by the single `_properties` scanner. That matches the "one property scanner" mitigation in the WP's Risks section, and the comparison above shows behaviour is unchanged.\n\nThe scratch scripts I used are in `/tmp/oldyc/` (`cmp.py`, `adv.py`, `adv2.py`, plus a copy of the old `yamlcore.py`), outside the repo. I made no repository edits.
