tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; export NO_PROXY=127.0.0.1,localhost no_proxy=127.0.0.1,localhost\nT=$(mktemp -d); mkdir -p $T/srv/prompts $T/.troupe\nprintf 'model: opus\\ntemperature: 0.7\\ntools: [a, b]\\nprompt: file:prompts/sys.md\\nextra: http://127.0.0.1:18765/x.yaml\\n' > $T/srv/team.yaml\nprintf 'hello sys\\n' > $T/srv/prompts/sys.md\nprintf 'deep: 1\\n' > $T/srv/x.yaml\nprintf 'temperature: 0.7\\nmodel: gpt\\n' > $T/srv/coder.yaml\nprintf 'a: http://127.0.0.1:18765/cyc2.yaml\\n' > $T/srv/cyc1.yaml\nprintf 'b: http://127.0.0.1:18765/cyc1.yaml\\n' > $T/srv/cyc2.yaml\nprintf '\\xff\\xfe' > $T/srv/bad.md\n(cd $T/srv && exec python3 -m http.server 18765 --bind 127.0.0.1 >$T/srv.log 2>&1) &\necho $! > /tmp/probe_srv.pid; echo $T > /tmp/probe_T; sleep 1\ncat > $T/.troupe/troupe.yml <<'EOF'\nteam: http://127.0.0.1:18765/team.yaml  # shared\ncoder:\n  $ref: http://127.0.0.1:18765/coder.yaml\n  temperature: 0.3\nwebhook: https://hooks.example.com/notify\nEOF\ncat > $T/.troupe/troupe.schema.json <<'EOF'\n{\"$schema\": \"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema\",\"type\":\"object\",\n \"properties\":{\"webhook\":{\"type\":\"string\",\"format\":\"uri\"},\n  \"team\":{\"type\":\"object\",\"properties\":{\"model\":{\"type\":\"string\"},\"max_tokens\":{\"type\":\"integer\"}}}}}\nEOF\nexport TROUPE_THEATRE=$T\nr(){ echo \"\\$ $*\"; uv run --quiet troupe config \"$@\"; echo \"[exit $?]\"; }\nr get team.model; r get team.prompt; r get team.extra.deep; r get webhook; r show coder --sources; r show --sources\nr set team.model sonnet; r set team.extra.deep 2; r set team.tools.2 x; r set --in-include team.newkey 1; r set team '{a: 1}'\nr set coder.model x; r set coder.temperature 0.5\nr set team.max_tokens 2000; r set team.max_tokens lots; r set docs https://x.example/y; r set --include webhook https://h.example/n; r set webhook https://h.example/n\nr get --include team.model\ncat $T/.troupe/troupe.yml; grep -c GET $T/srv.log; grep -v \"team.yaml\\|coder.yaml\\|x.yaml\\|sys.md\"...[truncated 13 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; export NO_PROXY=127.0.0.1,localhost no_proxy=127.0.0.1,localhost; T=$(cat /tmp/probe_T); export TROUPE_THEATRE=$T\nr(){ echo \"\\$ $*\"; uv run --quiet troupe config \"$@\"; echo \"[exit $?]\"; }\n: > $T/srv.log; r set coder.temperature 0.6; cat $T/srv.log | awk '{print $7}'\ncp $T/.troupe/troupe.yml /tmp/probe_orig.yml\nrm $T/.troupe/troupe.schema.json\nw(){ printf \"$1\" > $T/.troupe/troupe.yml; }\nw 'a: http://127.0.0.1:18765/missing.yaml\\n'; r get a\nw 'a: http://127.0.0.1:18765/cyc1.yaml\\n'; r get a\nw 'a: http://127.0.0.1:18765/bad.md\\n'; r get a\nw 'a: http://127.0.0.1:18765/team.yaml\\nb: http://127.0.0.1:18765/team.yaml\\n'; : > $T/srv.log; r get b.model; awk '{print $7}' $T/srv.log\nprintf 'q: http://127.0.0.1:18765/missing2.yaml\\n' > $T/srv/nest.yaml\nw 'a: http://127.0.0.1:18765/nest.yaml\\n'; r get a\nw 'p: http://127.0.0.1:18765/prompts/sys.md?v=2\\n'; r get p\nw 'x: !literal {a: 1}\\n'; r get x\nw 'x: !foo 1\\n'; r get x\nw 'x:\\n  $ref: !literal file:p.md\\n'; r get x\nw '!literal k: 1\\n'; r get k\nw 'note: !literal file:readme.md\\ndocs: !literal https://x.example/y\\nn: !literal 5\\n'; r show; : > $T/srv.log; r get docs; wc -l < $T/srv.log\nw 'b: 1\\n'; echo hi > $T/.troupe/p.md; r set b file:p.md; cat $T/.troupe/troupe.yml; r set --include b file:p.md; cat $T/.troupe/troupe.yml; r get b\nw 'docs: !literal https://old.example # c\\n'; r set docs plain-text; cat $T/.troupe/troupe.yml; r set --include docs file:p.md; cat $T/.troupe/troupe.yml\nw 'b: 1\\n'; r set --include team http://127.0.0.1:18765/missing.yaml; cat $T/.troupe/troupe.yml\nr set agents '{coder: {docs: https://x.example/y, n: 1}}'; cat $T/.troupe/troupe.yml\nr set x '!literal a'"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; export NO_PROXY=127.0.0.1,localhost no_proxy=127.0.0.1,localhost; T=$(cat /tmp/probe_T); export TROUPE_THEATRE=$T\ncat > /tmp/probe_srv2.py <<'EOF'\nimport http.server, time, sys\nclass H(http.server.BaseHTTPRequestHandler):\n    def log_message(self,*a): sys.stderr.write(self.command+\" \"+self.path+\"\\n\")\n    def do_GET(self):\n        p=self.path\n        if p.startswith(\"/redir-file\"): self.send_response(302); self.send_header(\"Location\",\"file:///etc/passwd\"); self.end_headers(); return\n        if p.startswith(\"/redir-ftp\"): self.send_response(301); self.send_header(\"Location\",\"ftp://x/y.yaml\"); self.end_headers(); return\n        if p.startswith(\"/redir-ok\"): self.send_response(302); self.send_header(\"Location\",\"/anch.yaml\"); self.end_headers(); return\n        if p.startswith(\"/trickle\"):\n            self.send_response(200); self.send_header(\"Content-Length\",\"100\"); self.end_headers()\n            for i in range(100): self.wfile.write(b\"a\"); self.wfile.flush(); time.sleep(1)\n            return\n        if p.startswith(\"/anch.yaml\"):\n            b=b\"base: &b {x: 1}\\nal: *b\\nlist: [1]\\n\"; self.send_response(200); self.send_header(\"Content-Length\",str(len(b))); self.end_headers(); self.wfile.write(b); return\n        if p.startswith(\"/esc.yaml\"):\n            b=b\"p: file:file:///etc/passwd\\n\"; self.send_response(200); self.send_header(\"Content-Length\",str(len(b))); self.end_headers(); self.wfile.write(b); return\n        if p.startswith(\"/esc2.yaml\"):\n            b=b\"p: file://evil.example/x\\n\"; self.send_response(200); self.send_header(\"Content-Length\",str(len(b))); self.end_headers(); self.wfile.write(b); return\n        self.send_response(404); self.end_headers()\nhttp.server.ThreadingHTTPServer((\"127.0.0.1\",18766),H).serve_forever()\nEOF\npython3 /tmp/probe_srv2.py 2>$T/srv2.log & echo $! > /tmp/probe_srv2.pid; sleep 1\nr(){ echo \"\\$ $*\"; uv run --quiet troupe config \"$...[truncated 578 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; export NO_PROXY=127.0.0.1,localhost no_proxy=127.0.0.1,localhost; T=$(cat /tmp/probe_T); export TROUPE_THEATRE=$T\nr(){ echo \"\\$ $*\"; uv run --quiet troupe config \"$@\"; echo \"[exit $?]\"; }\nw(){ printf \"$1\" > $T/.troupe/troupe.yml; }\ns(){ printf '%s' \"$1\" > $T/.troupe/troupe.schema.json; }\nw 'model: 5\\nwebhook: not a uri\\n'\ns '{'; r get model\ns '{\"$ref\":\"https://example.com/s.json\"}'; r get model\ns '{\"$schema\":\"http://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#\"}'; r get model\ns '{\"type\":3}'; r get model\ns '{\"properties\":{\"model\":{\"type\":\"string\"}}}'; r get model\ns '{\"properties\":{\"webhook\":{\"format\":\"uri\"}}}'; r get webhook\n: > $T/srv.log\nw 'agents:\\n  coder:\\n    urls: [http://127.0.0.1:18765/u1]\\nr: http://127.0.0.1:18765/r1\\na: http://127.0.0.1:18765/a1\\np: {x: http://127.0.0.1:18765/p1}\\n'\ns '{\"properties\":{\"agents\":{\"additionalProperties\":{\"properties\":{\"urls\":{\"items\":{\"format\":\"uri\"}}}}},\"r\":{\"$ref\":\"#/$defs/u\"},\"a\":{\"allOf\":[{\"format\":\"uri\"}]},\"p\":{\"patternProperties\":{\"x\":{\"format\":\"uri\"}}}},\"$defs\":{\"u\":{\"format\":\"uri\"}}}'\nr get agents.coder.urls.0; awk '{print $7}' $T/srv.log\n# alias of uri-literal stays literal\nw 'webhook: &w https://x.example/y\\nother: *w\\n'; s '{\"properties\":{\"webhook\":{\"format\":\"uri\"}}}'; r get other\n# load-time violation in remote content\nprintf 'model: 5\\n' > $T/srv/m5.yaml; w 'team: http://127.0.0.1:18765/m5.yaml\\n'; s '{\"properties\":{\"team\":{\"properties\":{\"model\":{\"type\":\"string\"}}}}}'; r get team.model\nrm $T/.troupe/troupe.schema.json\n# no write/events on refusal\nw 'team: http://127.0.0.1:18765/team.yaml\\n'; r get team.model; ls $T/.troupe; r history | wc -l; r set team.model x; r history | wc -l"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\nimport json,re,subprocess\nd=json.load(open('spec/troupe-config-remote-includes-01M4BMJ5/acceptance-matrix.json'))\nfor c in d['criteria']:\n    ev=c['evidence'].split(' — ')[0]\n    print(c['criterion_id'],c['pass_fail'],'|',ev[:300])\n    for name in re.findall(r'\\btest_[a-z0-9_]+',ev):\n        if name.startswith('test_') and not name.endswith('.py'):\n            r=subprocess.run(['grep','-rln',f'def {name}\\\\b','tests'],capture_output=True,text=True)\n            if not r.stdout: print('   MISSING',name)\nEOF"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; export NO_PROXY=127.0.0.1,localhost no_proxy=127.0.0.1,localhost; T=$(mktemp -d); mkdir -p $T/.troupe; export TROUPE_THEATRE=$T\nr(){ echo \"\\$ $*\"; uv run --quiet troupe config \"$@\"; echo \"[exit $?]\"; }\nprintf 'a: 1\\n' > $T/.troupe/troupe.yml\nr set --include b file:nope.md; cat $T/.troupe/troupe.yml\nr set b http://127.0.0.1:1/x.yaml; cat $T/.troupe/troupe.yml; r get b\nprintf 'r: http://127.0.0.1:1/x.yaml\\nz: 1\\n' > $T/.troupe/troupe.yml; r set z 2\n# schema order: document order vs alpha\nprintf 'zeta: 1\\nalpha: 1\\n' > $T/.troupe/troupe.yml; printf '{\"properties\":{\"zeta\":{\"type\":\"string\"},\"alpha\":{\"type\":\"string\"}}}' > $T/.troupe/troupe.schema.json; r get zeta\n# --include combined with --in-include\nrm $T/.troupe/troupe.schema.json; echo 'k: 1' > $T/.troupe/inc.yaml; echo hi > $T/.troupe/p.md; printf 'i: file:inc.yaml\\n' > $T/.troupe/troupe.yml; r set --in-include --include --reason why i.p file:p.md; cat $T/.troupe/inc.yaml; r get i.p\nrm -rf $T"}

`apply.py:239`, `_scalar_edits`/`_retag` `:478-586`; `src/troupe/writeback/scalars.py:148`; `blocks.py:192`; `aliases.py:281-286` | `tests/integration/test_set_literal.py`; `tests/unit/test_literal_rendering.py`; `test_set_include_remote.py` |\n| NFR-001 only reads sent | `remote.py:81` (GET only) | Request-log asserts in `test_remote_readonly.py:55,127` and `test_placement_remote.py:77` |\n| NFR-002 refusals change nothing | Load happens before history opens; `fileio` checks every source before writing any | `test_remote_acceptance.py`, `test_set_schema.py`; my probe showed the history count unchanged after a refusal |\n| NFR-003 byte-surgical writes | `_retag` / `_smallest` (`apply.py:542-598`) | Whole-file comparisons in `test_set_literal.py` and `test_remote_readonly.py` |\n| NFR-004 10 s bound | `_Deadline` | `test_remote_fetch.py` trickle/hang; my trickle probe ended at 10 s |\n| NFR-005 tests and gates | — | Left to your full-suite run |\n| C-001 / C-002 / C-005 | `contracts/cli.md` records choices separately; `atomic_write` is the only writer (`fileio.py:87`) | Existing tests |\n| C-003 earlier behaviour kept, except S-13 | `test_set_existing.py` was updated to pass `--include` | Yes |\n| C-004 errors name path, file or URL | All new messages checked (one partial, see D-2) | Yes |\n| C-006 offline tests | `tests/remote_server.py` on 127.0.0.1:0 | Yes |\n\n## Drift findings\n\n- **D-1 (Low): the first schema violation is picked by key name, not document order.** `src/troupe/schema.py:223-228` (`_order`) sorts keys alphabetically. research R-6 says "smallest JSON path in document order". Probe: with `zeta: 1` then `alpha: 1` and both required to be strings, the error names `alpha`. The output is still deterministic, and the spec (S-8 "first failure") doesn't define the order, so either the research text or `_order` should change.\n- **D-2 (Low): one refusal lacks the path and the "included from" wording.** `src/troupe/includes.py:72`: a `file:` in remote content that doesn't resolve to http(s) gives `include 'file:file:///etc/passwd' in http://…/esc.yaml does not resolve to an http(s) URL`. It names the URL (C-004 is met) but not the configuration path, unlike every other load error.\n- **D-3 (Low, spec vs contract): nested fetch failures name a URL, not a local file.** When a fetch fails inside remote content, the message says "included from <remote URL>" (`loader.py:496`). spec S-2 / FR-003 say stderr names "the local file holding the include". `contracts/cli.md` explicitly allows "<local or remote including location>". The code follows the contract; spec.md should be aligned.\n- **D-4 (Low, artefacts):**\n  - Every row of `acceptance-matrix.json` still has the placeholder description "Verify FR-00x is satisfied" and the note "TODO: replace with a real acceptance criterion".\n  - The matrix covers only the FRs; NFRs, constraints and S-1 to S-13 have no rows.\n  - `spec.md` still says Status "Draft", and every FR/NFR row says "Open".\n- **Exact contract messages checked via the CLI:** remote refusal (em dash, line N, outermost include, `$ref` line 3); fetch errors for 404, non-UTF-8, `file:` redirect, `ftp:` redirect, timeout and connection refused; URL cycle; all four schema-file error kinds; load-time and `set`-time violations; `--include` on a `uri` path; `--include` with an unreadable target; the bad-tag messages for a mapping, another tag, `$ref` and a key; VALUE tag → exit 2; `--include` on `get` → exit 2. All match `contracts/cli.md`.\n\n## Risks and security\n\n- **Network access is confined to `remote.py`.** No other module imports `urllib.request`, `http.client` or `socket`. `includes.py` uses only `urllib.parse`.\n- **TLS:** `ssl.create_default_context()` at `remote.py:331`, and the handshake honours the deadline (`:231-236`). The untrusted-certificate test runs because `openssl` is present.\n- **Redirects:** only http/https targets are followed (`remote.py:363-382`). Probes confirmed `file://` and `ftp://` redirects are refused.\n  - An https → http downgrade redirect is allowed. The contract permits http, so this is Low; it belongs with 1j5.4.\n- **No writes to remote content.** `atomic_write` is the only file writer (`fileio.py:87`), and it is reached only through `write_files`, which checks every source before writing any. Both checkpoints were confirmed by probes: existing value, nested remote, list item, `--in-include`, whole replacement, an alias inside remote content with and without `--expand-alias`, and the `$ref` side of a merged view.\n- **Schema `$ref` is never fetched:** the walk at `schema.py:157-163` requires fragments, and the validator gets an empty `Registry()` (`:63`). A probe confirmed a non-fragment `$ref` exits 4.\n- **Escaping to the local disk from remote content is blocked** (`includes.py:71`). However, `file://evil.example/x` inside remote content resolves (standard urljoin) to `http://evil.example/x` on another host. That matches S-1, and remote content can already name any URL directly, so it adds no capability. Worth one line in the docs.\n- **Proxies:** proxies from the environment are honoured (`remote.py:389`). Credentials embedded in a proxy URL would be sent to the proxy. Low; this is outside S-2's "no credentials to the server".\n- **Watch:** while a remote include keeps failing, each watch tick can block for up to 10 s while holding the theatre lock (`watch.py`, S-6), which stalls other commands. Low.\n- **Known open tracker, confirmed still present:**\n  - 1j5.1: the socket is created outside the per-address `try` at `remote.py:250`.\n  - 1j5.2: `mark_literals` runs at `apply.py:239`, before the remote checks at `:251`.\n  - 1j5.3: no body-size limit (`remote.py:84-87`).\n  - 1j5.4: http traffic is clear text with no warning.\n\n## Integration\n\n- The new modules all have live callers: `configuration` is used by `session.py:24` and `apply.py:101`; `includes` by `loader.py:74`, `apply.py:103`, `scalars.py:17`, `blocks.py:57` and `aliases.py:41`; `remote` by `loader.py:74`; `schema` by `loader`, `includes`, `configuration`, `session`, `apply` and `watch`; `location` by `tree`, `yamlout`, `placement` and `apply`.\n- The old include-prefix logic is gone from the loader. There is no `_is_include_string`, and no `"file:"` or `startswith("http` outside `includes.py`. Every include decision goes through `includes.include_kind` / `reads_as_include` (`loader.py:306,426,534`).\n- Fetch-once per command works: a `set` fetched each of its 4 URLs exactly once across its load and reloads, and the same URL included twice was fetched once.\n\n## Charter (TROUPE-001 to 009)\n\n- **001 byte preservation:** compliant. Tag insert/remove is a single smallest edit and keeps the comment column (probe: `docs: !literal plain-text          # c`).\n- **002 conservative choices:** compliant (refuse on uri + `--include`, on unfetchable new includes, on non-fragment `$ref`).\n- **003 errors name the path/file/URL:** compliant, with the partial gap in D-2.\n- **005 real tests:** compliant. The tests use a real HTTP server, CLI subprocesses and the history store.\n- **007 one decision per place:** compliant (`includes`, `schema.uri_at`, `load_configuration`, `remote_refusal`).\n- **004 platform and 008 (decisions recorded, never amended):** compliant.\n- **006 and 009:** these are process items for your full suite run and the commit history.\n\n## Recommended follow-ups\n\n1. Make `schema._order` use document order, or change research R-6 to say "keys by name" (D-1).\n2. Add "(in <url>, at <path>)" to the refusal at `includes.py:72` (D-2).\n3. Align spec S-2 / FR-003 wording with the contract on nested fetch failures (D-3).\n4. Replace the placeholder text in `acceptance-matrix.json`, add rows for NFRs, constraints and S-1 to S-13, and set spec status (D-4).\n5. Fix 1j5.1. It's a small change and stops a whole fetch failing on hosts with IPv6 disabled.\n6. Decide 1j5.2 (check the planned file before `mark_literals` so the S-4 message wins), 1j5.3 (size limit) and 1j5.4. Fold the https → http downgrade into 1j5.4.\n7. Optional: note in the docs that `file://host/...` inside remote content resolves to another host.

assistant: Step 7 (remote includes and a schema) is merged into `main` at `bc8d285`. Nothing was pushed and no pull request was opened.\n\n**Checks:** the full suite on the merged code passed (2674 tests, none skipped, so the TLS certificate test ran too), and mypy and ruff are clean. `main`'s tree is identical to that tested tree, and a smoke run on `main` passed.\n\n**What it does:**\n- **Remote includes:** a string starting `https://` or `http://` is now an include, fetched when the configuration loads. A fetch never takes more than 10 seconds, certificates are verified, and redirects go to http(s) only. Any failure exits 4 and names the URL.\n- **Remote content is read-only:** changing a value that comes from a remote include exits 3 and quotes the include line to change instead. New keys under a remote include become local overrides.\n- **Schema:** if `.troupe/troupe.schema.json` exists, the configuration must match it. A mismatch exits 4 when loading, and exits 3 for a `set` that would break it. Settings the schema marks `"format": "uri"`, and strings tagged `!literal`, are never treated as includes.\n- **`set`:** writes include-looking strings as `!literal …` unless `--include` is given.\n- **One behaviour change you should know about:** `set b file:p.md` used to create an include; it now writes `b: !literal file:p.md`. The contract requires this, and the two existing tests that relied on the old behaviour now pass `--include`.\n\n**How it went:**\n- **Reviews:** all 10 work packages were reviewed separately. Four were rejected once each and then fixed; three of those defects mattered:\n  - A fetch could run past 10 seconds.\n  - A loader change let a `set` silently change an unrelated value through a shared YAML anchor.\n  - Read-only refusals could come with the wrong error message.\n- **Final review:** the post-merge review gave "pass with notes". I fixed the spec and research wording it flagged; the code findings are filed as issues below.\n- **Planning-document fixes:** during implementation, three expectations in my own planning documents turned out to contradict the spec. I fixed those documents.\n- **Choices I made where the brief was silent:** these are recorded in the spec as S-1 to S-13. They include resolving `file:` includes inside remote files against the URL, and adding the `jsonschema` library for validation.\n\n**Needs your decision:**\n- `1j5.3`: there is no limit on how large a fetched remote file can be.\n- `1j5.4`: `http://` includes travel unencrypted with no warning, and an `https` include can be redirected to `http`.\n\n**Smaller follow-ups:** issues `1j5.1`, `1j5.2`, `1j5.5` and `1j5.6` (fetch fallback on IPv6-disabled hosts, an error-message ordering edge case, one error message missing its path, and `watch` holding a lock up to 10 s while a remote include keeps failing). The parent issue `1j5` stays open only because these six are its children.\n\nOne leftover: method couldn't delete the mission's coordination branch because its worktree is still checked out. Earlier missions left theirs in place too; I've left it.

assistant: I chose a 1 MiB size limit, no warning for `http://` includes, and refusing `https` → `http` redirects. All three are merged into `main` (`f172d52`), not pushed. The full suite passed (2682 tests, including 8 new ones) and mypy and ruff are clean.\n\n**Size limit (1j5.3): a remote include can be at most 1 MiB.** A larger file is a failed fetch: exit 4, with stderr `cannot fetch <url> (included from <file>, at <path>): larger than 1 MiB, the limit for a remote include`.\n- **Why 1 MiB:** your configurations are under 1,000 leaves in under 50 files, so a real include is a few kilobytes and this limit is far above anything legitimate. Without a limit, a broken or hostile server could make troupe read an unbounded body into memory. Refusing can't lose or corrupt data, and a failed fetch is already contracted as exit 4.\n- **How it's enforced:** the declared size is checked first, then the limit is enforced again while reading. A missing or false `Content-Length` can't get past it.\n\n**No warning for `http://` includes (1j5.4).**\n- The contract makes `http://` a normal include, and you write it on purpose.\n- A warning would add stderr noise to every successful `get`, `show` and `set` that harnesses read, with no contract basis.\n\n**Refuse `https` → `http` redirects (1j5.4).**\n- It counts as a failed fetch: exit 4, stderr `redirect to <url> refused (https may not redirect to http)`, and the `http` URL is never contacted. Every hop of a redirect chain is checked.\n- `http` → `https` and same-scheme redirects still work.\n- **Why:** an `https://` include asks for an encrypted source, and silently following a downgrade would remove that protection. If you want plain http, you write an `http://` include.\n\nThese are recorded in the spec as agent choices S-14 to S-16, with the contract's message table, FR-003 and research updated to match. Issues `1j5.3` and `1j5.4` are closed.\n\nFour smaller follow-ups remain open under the parent issue `1j5`:\n- `1j5.1`: a fetch fails outright instead of falling back to IPv4 on hosts with IPv6 disabled.\n- `1j5.2`: an error-message ordering edge case with `--include`.\n- `1j5.5`: one refusal message is missing the configuration path.\n- `1j5.6`: `watch` holds a lock for up to 10 s while a remote include keeps failing.
